**What the Scan flow deliberately does NOT do.** It does not complete a scan.
Camera, Gallery and File hand off to NATIVE pickers (`ImageCropPicker.openCamera`
/ `openPicker`, Expo `DocumentPicker`), which put a system sheet on top of the
app. In a serial suite a system sheet that fails to dismiss does not fail one
flow, it strands every flow after it — and there is no Health fixture in
`src/services/e2e-document-pick.ts` to feed a deterministic image (its
`VALID_KEYS` are Kaizen-only). `health-scan-run` with shots, both review cards
and `handleSaveFood` stay at the unit layer until that fixture exists.

## Flagged / deferred (not scored)

Do **not** mark Pass/Fail for these in RESULTS until product ships the feature or harness exists.

### Blockers — no verdict possible today

| Topic | Why flagged | Target phase / harness |
|-------|-------------|-------------------------|
| **The Maestro global lock had no staleness check — one killed run wedged the whole machine** | `scripts/e2e/maestro-global-lock.sh` releases through an EXIT trap, which does **not** run on SIGKILL — and an OOM kill is precisely what the lock exists to prevent, so that is the common exit, not the rare one. A dead owner therefore held the lock until the 7200 s timeout, and the wait was SILENT: the runner produced zero output and looked hung. Found by hitting it — a lock owned by pid `32766` from 04:58 blocked a 09:08 Health run and a 09:29 one, four and five hours later; neither executed a single command, and both were eventually killed. Every suite is affected: `run-health-suite.sh`, `run-budget-suite.sh`, `run-kaizen-suite.sh`, `run-language-suite.sh` and `run-house-suite.sh` all source it | **FIXED 2026-07-26** — the acquire loop now reads the owner pid, `kill -0`s it, and breaks the lock when the owner is gone (logging what it broke); a lock dir with no owner file yet is treated as a live race and still waited on. It also prints who it is waiting for every 60 s, so a real wait can no longer be mistaken for a hang. Verified on the next run: `Maestro global lock: breaking stale lock held by a dead process (32766 run-health-suite.sh health)` |
| **`hideKeyboard` was still on EIGHTEEN numeric fields — most of the suite could not have finished** | The 2026-07-26 root cause ("it is the keyboard TYPE, not luck") was correct and was applied to three flows; the entry then claimed "the remaining call sites are text-keyboard-only and are left alone until they are next touched." **That claim was false.** An audit that resolves every `- hideKeyboard` back to the field the flow last focused, and that field back to its `keyboardType` in `src/features/health`, found **21 of 49 sites on a numeric keyboard** — including `vitality-log-and-score` (`number-pad`), `weight-tab-log-and-edit`, `weight-tab-dashboard-controls` (all three goal fields), `nutrition-log-meal` (×2), `trends-charts`, `food-library-manage` (×2), `recipes-build-and-scale` (×4), `fridge-stock-and-expiry`, `body-measure-and-trend` (×2), `more-overflow-body-habits` and `exercises-detail-log`. `hideKeyboard` fails the FLOW, not the step, so each one is a whole surface lost | **FIXED 2026-07-26** — all eighteen `decimal-pad` / `number-pad` sites converted to `subflows/dismiss-health-keypad.yaml` with a card-label `KEYPAD_ANCHOR`. Anchors were checked to be non-touchable: `weight-tab-dashboard-controls` uses `Height \(cm\)` (a `GoalField` label) because the card's own `GOAL & BODY DETAILS` heading is a `Pressable` that would COLLAPSE the panel — exactly what the subflow's contract warns about. The five sites left on `hideKeyboard` are `numbers-and-punctuation` (`health-fridge-expiry-input`, `health-injury-date-input`), which on iOS is a full keyboard WITH a return key and a standard dismiss |
| ~~**The largest remaining control gap is `HealthVitalityScreen` 5/18**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-07-26.** `vitality-scales-and-toggles.yaml` (`HEALTH-VITALITY-120 … 134`) takes it to **18/18**: all ten `HealthScaleRow`s driven to two levels each, `health-vitality-quality` to both ENDS of its range, all five `HealthToggleRow`s flipped ON and OFF, and the kegel field at its ceiling (`99` → clamped `50`) and at empty. The two assertions worth copying elsewhere are the DERIVED ones — `energyScore` and `mentalScore` are pure functions of controls on that screen and of nothing else, so the flow predicts `Energy: 40` / `Mental: 70` and then `Energy: 70` / `Mental: 55` exactly, which a regression that repainted the segment track without persisting would fail. `sexualHealthScore` and `erectionScore` are deliberately NOT asserted absolutely: both subtract for the ISSUE toggles, which live on a shared account this flow does not own | done — next by size: `HealthRecipesScreen`'s LOG TO DIARY panel (4), then `HealthDashboardCards` on Home |
| **Row verbs were being driven by accessibility label ALONE** | `food-library-manage`, `recipes-build-and-scale` and `fridge-stock-and-expiry` selected every per-row action with a bare `tapOn: {text: 'Delete Maestro Oats'}`. That works, and it is why those controls were on device while the inventory scored them undriven — but a bare label match will happily land on any other element carrying the same string, and it cannot fail when the control is RENAMED, only when the copy changes | **FIXED 2026-07-26** — 21 taps across the three flows now name the id pattern AND the label together (`id: 'health-food-delete-.*'` + `text: 'Delete Maestro Oats'`), the shape the nutrition flows already used. Both must match the same element, so the confirm alert's own `Delete` button still cannot satisfy a row verb, and a renamed control now fails loudly. This closed `HealthFridgeScreen` at 16/16 and moved Foods 13→17 and Recipes 10→15 |
| ~~Two flows still use the `enabled:` selector~~ | **CLOSED 2026-07-26** — `recipes-build-and-scale.yaml` and `fridge-stock-and-expiry.yaml` converted to behavioural proofs, per the row below. Recipes presses the disabled `health-recipe-add-ingredient` and asserts `health-recipe-no-foods` survives; the fridge presses the disabled save button and asserts `health-fridge-name-input` STILL holds `Maestro Milk` — `handleSave` calls `resetForm()` on success, so the name surviving is the proof nothing was filed | done |
| ~~No unit layer for the Cycle store~~ / ~~No unit layer for the Vitality store~~ | **BOTH CLOSED** — `src/features/health/__tests__/healthCycleStorage.test.ts` and `healthVitalityStorage.test.ts` both exist and run green inside the 44-suite / 1825-test Health run at 100% on all four coverage measures. The two rows above them in this table said otherwise and were stale | What is still outstanding is only the BACKFILL of numbered `HEALTH-CYCLE-001…` / `HEALTH-VITALITY-001…` rows into this document — the tests exist, the table entries do not |
| **A second session was running this suite while these flows were being written** | The Maestro global lock (`scripts/e2e/maestro-global-lock.sh`) serialises runs, so a device run started here queued behind another agent's `nutrition-meal-detail-tools` run already in flight. That is the lock working — but it also means twelve flow files were EDITED while a suite was mid-execution, and Maestro reads each YAML at flow start, so a later flow in that other run picked up edits its earlier flows did not have | Same answer as the concurrent-development row below: run against a quiesced tree, or a `git worktree` with its own Metro port and its own lock name. Worth making the lock refuse to start when the working tree under `e2e/maestro/health` has uncommitted changes newer than the run |
| `scroll-all-screens.yaml` hard-fails | Unlike the other flows it calls `launch-health.yaml`, which waits 120 s for `health-home-screen` and then fails instead of skipping | Wrap in the same `when: visible` gate as the other flows |
| ~~**`GET /health/sync` 500s in the backend suite**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-07-25, same day it was found.** `HealthService.sync()` reads the P2 tables (`custom_foods`, `recipes`, `injuries`, `fridge_items`, `user_files`, `widget_preferences`, `activity_notification_preferences`), but `health.test.ts` and `health-sync.test.ts` seeded with `createHealthTables()` alone — the 0119 set only — so `GET /sync` 500'd: **4 + 4 red tests**. `health-test-helpers.ts` now creates the P2 tables for those suites; re-verified **109/109 green**. Left recorded because the class of bug recurs on every migration: a P1 suite's fixture is a silent dependency of every later phase | done — but confirm `0120_health_p2.sql` is applied to Health **staging and production** D1 before P2 is called shipped; a green miniflare run proves nothing about a D1 that never got the migration |

### Product / code defects surfaced by this audit (fix, don't just test)

| Topic | Why flagged | Target phase / harness |
|-------|-------------|-------------------------|
| **The "on-device only" story is stale, matrix-wide** (PRIV-018, API-029, §0 Network surface, HOME-004, MORE-012/013) | P1 moved the whole health domain onto `symply-health-api` (`backend/src/routes/health.ts` at `/health/*`, migration 0119, `healthRepository.ts`), and P2 added three more routers plus R2 file storage. The matrix still asserts "every health log is MMKV-local and issues no request" and API-029 still frames four 404s as "the executable form of the privacy promise" that "fails loudly the day a health data-plane route ships" — that day was P1. API-029's four probe paths (`/health-log`, `/weights`, `/water`, `/health/entries`) are not the paths that shipped (`/health/weight/entries`, `/health/water`, …), so the test still **passes while its stated intent is false**. Not corrected here because the fix is a product decision, not a doc edit: either the UI copy is wrong or the privacy contract changed | Decide the posture, then rewrite the PRIV family + API-029 + the Home/More privacy copy rows in one pass. Until then, do not cite PRIV-018 or API-029 as evidence of anything |
| ~~**The keypad covered the field you were typing into, on every section tab**~~ | **FOUND AND FIXED 2026-07-26 — member-facing, and the same defect class as the Home fix.** `HealthSectionScreen` — the shared chrome behind Nutrition, Activity, Trends, Body, Habits, Cycle, Vitality, Foods, Recipes, Fridge, Injuries and Weight — set `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"` but **not** `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets`. Caught by screenshot while triaging something else: tapping the "kcal" box in Nutrition's "Or log calories only" put the decimal pad's top edge ABOVE the row, so the member could not see the number they were typing and the button beside it was unreachable. `HealthHomeScreen` had had exactly this fixed days earlier (the keypad's top edge at ≈568pt over a log row at y≈569pt) — the fix had simply not been generalised to the shared container, which is the fleet rule. One line, no new branch: `src/features/health/components/HealthSectionScreen.tsx`, `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets` on the ScrollView. `src/features/health` re-verified at 100%/100%/100%/100%, 1825 tests, 44 suites | done |
| **Health data survives sign-out** (PRIV-011, MORE-029) | `health.*` MMKV keys are global, not per-user, and no logout path clears them | Clear health keys on logout **or** namespace them per user id |
| **Cross-user leak on a shared device** (PRIV-012) | Consequence of the above: user B sees user A's weight, water and notes | Same fix; verify with a 2nd seeded account |
| **Refresh token never revoked on sign-out** (MORE-028) | `authApi.logout` → `POST /auth/logout` exists but has **zero call sites**; the token is dropped locally and stays valid server-side | Wire `logout()` to call the endpoint (fleet-wide — affects every brand) |
| **Unhandled storage failures** (HOME-058, 060, 061, MORE-026 — HOME-059 RETIRED 2026-08-01 with the Home weight-write path) | `hydrate`, `handleWater`, `handleSaveNote` and the More prefs load have no `catch`. A read failure pins the spinner forever; write failures lose data silently. (The weight-write failure this row used to also cite, `handleLog`/HOME-059, no longer applies to Home — there is no Home weight write left to fail. The equivalent gap, if any, now belongs to the Weight tab's `WeightEntryEditor` save handler, not audited here) | Add error states + retry |
| **Weight entry id collision** (STORE-017) | `id = loggedAt` ISO string → same-millisecond entries share an id and delete together | Use a uuid |
| No way to clear health data (PRIV-015) | Only per-entry weight deletion exists; water/notes/prefs cannot be cleared from the UI at all | Data-portability milestone |
| **Health widget is non-functional** (WIDGET-011) | `HealthWidgetData.load()` gates on `WidgetStore.isAuthenticated`, which needs a non-empty `auth_token` — but `WidgetSyncModule.setAuth` **deletes** that key and `app/_layout.tsx:173` passes an empty token. The gate is always false, so the widget renders the empty state even with a good snapshot. **Budget has the identical bug**; Kaizen and Language don't gate and work | Either drop the auth gate (matching Kaizen/Language) or write a real token — decide fleet-wide, it is not Health-specific |
| **Health widget receives 2 of 6 fields** (WIDGET-022) | `SymplyHealthWidgetContent` declares `steps`, `steps_goal`, `water_ml`, `water_goal_ml`, `move_pct`, `next_reminder`; `HealthHomeScreen.tsx:116` writes only the two water fields. Steps tile is permanently `—`, no move %, no reminder | Either write the missing fields or shrink the widget to what the shell actually tracks |
| **`watch_health_today` has no writer** (WATCH-005) | The Health watch glance reads a key nothing in the repo writes; the JS bridge emits `widget_*` only. Same for Budget, Kaizen, Language — all four non-House glances are empty by construction | Add a producer **or** retire the view. Resolve the WATCH-015 schema fork first |

But before this pass, **zero** `HEALTH-GOALS-*` rows existed in any table, there was no `## GOALS` section, no TOC entry, and no Execution-Groups row — a reader trusting the Revision log's own claims (this matrix's most-cited evidence of rigor) would believe 37+ rows were scored when the true number was 0. This is the same failure mode the 2026-07-28 entry ITSELF caught and fixed for `HEALTH-ONB-019…022` ("the entry immediately above claimed they existed but the table rows were never actually written") — except for GOALS, the correction never happened. **Not a fabricated feature**: the gap was between "described in prose" and "actually tabulated," not between "claimed" and "real" | Closed 2026-07-31 by writing the real `GOALS` section (35 rows, matched against both flows' actual `E2E_MATRIX_ROW` tags and `HealthGoalsScreen.tsx` source). Going forward: a Revision entry claiming new row IDs should be written in the SAME edit as the rows themselves, never as a promise to backfill |
| **P2 — the GOALS screen's Jest suite and its Maestro flows independently claim the SAME `HEALTH-GOALS-1xx` ID range for DIFFERENT behaviour** | `HealthGoalsScreen.test.tsx` embeds `it('HEALTH-GOALS-102: an unset weight goal hydrates its fields BLANK...')`; the Maestro flow's `E2E_MATRIX_ROW: HEALTH-GOALS-102` is "calorie preset picked, manual figure typed." Every number below 130 collides between the two files with an unrelated meaning attached, because two different authors independently started counting from 101 for the same section prefix without checking the other layer had already claimed it — the opposite of how every other section in this matrix works (one numeric block per layer, e.g. `ACT` 001…024 store / 050…057 screen / 101+ E2E). This matrix's new `GOALS` section adopts the Maestro numbers as authoritative and does not reproduce the Jest suite's colliding numbers; that is a documentation-side mitigation, not a fix — the `it()` strings in `HealthGoalsScreen.test.tsx` and `healthGoalsStorage.test.ts` still say `HEALTH-GOALS-1xx` and will mislead the next person who greps the test file for a matrix ID | Renumber the Jest suite's embedded IDs onto an unused block (e.g. `001…050`, matching the `ACT`/`NUTR` convention) — an app-test-code change, out of scope for a matrix-only pass |
| **P2 — the `RESIL` section (2026-07-25) has six rows that violate this file's own `HEALTH-<PREFIX>-<NNN>` ID schema** | `HEALTH-VITAL-030…032` should read `HEALTH-VITALITY-` (the section used everywhere else for Men's Health); `EX-STORE-047…049, 067…069` is missing the mandatory `HEALTH-` prefix entirely and uses a hyphenated section code (`EX-STORE`) that appears nowhere in this file's Section→tag table. `HEALTH-SYNC-201`, `HEALTH-SCALE-001…005`, `HEALTH-REPO-016` and `HEALTH-CDASH-130…131` are all real, correctly-cited Unit rows but equally undeclared as section prefixes — none appears in the Section→Maestro-tag table near the top of this file or (until this pass) in the row-count breakdown. None of the six breaks a harness check (`healthSuiteCompleteness` only validates Maestro-flow testIDs, and these are all `Unit`-layer with no Maestro selector), so this is a citation-hygiene defect, not a functional one | Either fold `VITAL`→`VITALITY` and `EX-STORE`→`EXER`/`STORE` (a same-meaning rename, low risk since neither ID is referenced by any Maestro flow or code comment outside this file), or add all six as declared one-off Unit-only prefixes in the Section→tag table. Not renamed in this pass — renumbering a "stable once published" ID is its own citation risk, and neither collides with a differently-meaning ID the way the GOALS row above does |

### Control → flow coverage (2026-07-26, recounted a third time)

Built mechanically, not from memory: every JSX opening tag under
`src/features/health` carrying `onPress` / `onLongPress` / `onChangeText` /
`onValueChange` / `onSubmitEditing` / `onChange` / `onSelect` / `onToggle` /
`onConfirm` / `onRequestClose`, or being a `Pressable` / `TouchableOpacity` /
`TextInput` / `Switch` / `Modal`, matched against every id that any flow in
`e2e/maestro/health/**` **taps or types into** (`tapOn`, `inputText`,
`longPressOn`, `doubleTapOn`, `eraseText`) — assertions alone do not count as
driving a control.

Ids are counted **component-locally**: a backward-constructed id
(`` testID={`${testID}-close`} ``) is one entry against the component that
renders it, and it counts as driven when any of the base ids its own call sites
pass resolves to an id a flow drives. That resolution is what lets
`HealthMealTools` be scored at all — neither half of
`health-meal-copy-lunch-close` is written out in full anywhere in the source.

#### Two corrections to the counter, stated before the number

The previous count was reproduced exactly (**181**) before anything was changed,
so the two fixes below are measured against a verified baseline rather than
asserted. Both were counter defects, not coverage:

1. **The base half of a backward id was never credited.** The rule above was
   applied to the CHILD (`${testID}-${step}` on `HealthScaleRow`) and not to the
   PARENT that hands it the base. So `health-vitality-libido` was scored undriven
   while a flow was tapping `health-vitality-libido-8` — an id that only exists
   because the parent passed that base. There is no element with the bare id to
   tap: it is the row's container `View`. Driving `…-8` **is** driving that
   control, and the recount now says so.
2. **A base id can arrive through a prop that is not called `testID`.** The suite
   guard already records this (`screenTestID`, `idPrefix`); the counter did not.
   That alone is why `HealthBodyProgress` read `0/3` while
   `body-measure-and-trend.yaml` had been tapping `health-body-trend-metric-*`
   and `health-body-trend-range-30` since it was written.

Together those two credited **5** controls that flows were already driving
(`health-vitality-libido`, `-energy`, `health-cycle-mood`, `-energy`,
`health-exercise-detail`) plus `HealthBodyProgress`'s three and
`HealthWeightScreen`'s `ChoiceRow`. Both numbers are given below so the honest
delta is visible.

**262 interactive tags · 260 distinct testIDs · 226 driven (87%)**, from
**181 / 260 (70%)** at the start of this pass.

| Basis | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Counter as it stood (exact-literal only) | 181 (70%) | 213 (82%) |
| Counter corrected (base ids + prop aliases resolved) | 186 (72%) | **226 (87%)** |

The denominator is 260 rather than the 261 previously recorded; the difference is
one id in `HealthFoodLibraryScreen` that the earlier script double-counted. The
inventory script is `scratch`-only by design — it is re-derivable from this
description in a few minutes and would rot as a checked-in file.

**Twelve components are now at 100%**: `HealthActivityScreen`,
`HealthVitalityScreen`, `HealthCycleScreen`, `HealthBodyScreen`,
`HealthFridgeScreen`, `HealthExerciseDetailScreen`, `HealthWeightDashboard`,
`HealthBodyProgress`, `HealthMealTools`, `HealthHabitsScreen`,
`HealthWorkoutLibraryScreen`, `HealthTrendsScreen`.

### Harness gaps

> **2026-07-26 baseline run.** The suite was executed end to end for the first time. Read the two rows about the Home ScrollView and about concurrent development BEFORE triaging any Home failure: between them they account for every red flow in that run, and both produce failures that look like product regressions and are not. The single most misleading signature is `Element not found: health-log-weight-button` with a **Nutrition** screenshot — that is a tap landing on the tab bar, not a flow-ordering or state-leak bug.

| Topic | Why flagged | Target phase / harness |
|-------|-------------|-------------------------|
| **The P3 AI surface has NO Maestro flow, and one Home flow now contradicts the app** | `AI` ships 173 scored rows across backend vitest and Jest, and **zero** E2E: `e2e/maestro/health/` is owned by another agent, so `HealthCoachScreen` and `HealthScanScreen` are unproven on a device. Separately, `home-empty-and-privacy.yaml` (L111, L116) still asserts a COMING SOON row `health-upcoming-ai-coach` / `AI health coach` that the app **no longer renders** — the coach shipped, and UI_PARITY_AUDIT §7 item 10 says those rows live only until the feature does. `HEALTH-HOME-089` was updated in the same commit and now asserts the row's ABSENCE; the two yaml lines must come out for that flow to pass | Delete the two `health-upcoming-ai-coach` assertions from `home-empty-and-privacy.yaml`; then add `HEALTH-AI-3xx` E2E rows with `coach-consent-and-turn.yaml` + `scan-label-review.yaml` and the two `subflows/go-health-{coach,scan}.yaml` — both tabs are `defaultHidden`, so each flow must also pin its `tab-*` negative, and `more-overflow-all-tabs.yaml` is now **five** hidden tabs behind `brand.cjs` (Fridge, Workouts, Injuries, Coach, Scan) |
| No widget test target | `SymplyEcosystemWidgetExtension.xcscheme` has an empty `<Testables/>` and `project.pbxproj` defines no widget test bundle — every WIDGET Tier 2 row is unrunnable until one is added | Add `SymplyEcosystemWidgetTests` + a `test:widget` npm script |
| Watch tests not wired into CI | `scripts/run-watch-tests.sh` exists and the scheme lists both bundles, but nothing references it from `package.json` or `.github/workflows/` | Add `test:watch` and a CI job |
| ~~`run-health-flows-sequential.sh` duplicates the flow list~~ → **IT HAD SILENTLY DRIFTED** | The row used to read "a third place to edit when adding a flow". It was worse than an inconvenience: the hard-coded array held **21** flows while `config.yaml` `flowsOrder` holds **46**, so a "sequential run of the Health suite" skipped twenty-five flows — including every one added by the Weight, Habits, Injuries, Foods, Recipes, Fridge, Coach, Scan and diary passes — and then printed `SUMMARY 21/21 passed`. A green summary over two thirds of a suite is worse than a red one | **FIXED 2026-07-26** — the array is now parsed out of `config.yaml` `flowsOrder` with awk, preserving the order (which matters: the auth pair clears the Keychain and must run last), and the script hard-fails rather than reporting a vacuous pass if it parses zero flows. Verified: 46 flows, `home-weight-water-note` first, `login-email-submit-seeded` last. The fixed simulator UDID on line 44 is still hard-coded and is the remaining half of this row |
| **`routes/health-social.ts` has NO matrix rows** | A **P4** surface — family sharing, accountability buddies, community rooms, challenges and the scoped share grants that make any of it readable — is already imported and `app.route('/health', healthSocialRoutes)`-mounted at `backend/src/index.ts:237`, with 35 endpoints, `schema-health-social.ts` and `health-social-service.ts`. The only thing between it and live cross-user health sharing is the literal string `'true'` in `CONFIG_KV.health_social_enabled` — a deliberately inverted, deny-by-default kill switch (absent key, `'1'`, `'yes'`, `'FALSE'` all mean OFF). `health-social.test.ts` landed **while this pass was being written** and is still in flight (81 cases, **3 red** on the "an invite never reveals whether an email is registered" rule as of 2026-07-25). This matrix has **no rows** for any of it — it was out of the P2 scope, not covered | Land the in-flight suite green, then add a `SOCIAL` section here (kill switch in both directions, brand gate, auth, every `NEVER_SHAREABLE_SCOPES` entry) **and** the signed-off privacy review, before the flag is ever set. Until all three, treat the surface as unverified regardless of the flag |
| **Part of the P2 surface still has no UI** (`INJ`, and the file/R2 half of `ASSET`) | ~~no E2E for `FOOD`~~ **CLOSED 2026-07-25** — `food-library-manage.yaml`, `recipes-build-and-scale.yaml` and `fridge-stock-and-expiry.yaml` now drive the three P2 screens (groups 44–46), and the fridge gained `HEALTH-FRIDGE-101 … 113`. What remains: injuries, activity preferences and body insights have **no** screen and **no** client module, and the file/R2 surface has no screen either, so none of them has matrix rows beyond the backend contract — deliberately, rather than inventing them | Backfill `HEALTH-INJ-1xx` / `HEALTH-ASSET-1xx` E2E rows as each screen lands; each phase must update this matrix per PARITY_PLAN §6 |
| ~~**`more-overflow-all-tabs.yaml` is behind `brand.cjs`**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-07-26.** The sweep now covers all eight `defaultHidden` tabs — Body, Habits, Cycle, Vitality, Foods, Recipes, **Fridge**, Workouts — each with a row assertion, an opens-the-screen leg (`HEALTH-MORE-056` … `063`) and a `tab-*` negative. The class of gap is now caught mechanically: `e2e/__tests__/healthSuiteCompleteness.test.ts` fails in Jest when a flow drives a `testID` prefix the app never renders, or when a flow on disk is absent from `flowsOrder` | — |
| **The `avoid` injury gate cannot be seeded from the app** (EXER-111, EXER-113) | The workout library's safety behaviour — flag badges, the safety card, and the store REFUSING to log an `avoid` movement without an explicit acknowledgement — is driven entirely by `injury_flag` from `/health/exercises`, which the Worker derives from the account's active injuries. There is **no injury-logging surface in the app** (`INJ` is the P2 router with no UI), so an E2E run cannot create the precondition. Both flagged legs are therefore conditional: they assert hard when an account happens to carry an injury and skip otherwise, which on the seeded test account means they normally skip. The rule itself is pinned by `HealthWorkoutLibrary.test.tsx` and the backend suite | Either land the injuries screen (then make the legs unconditional and add a seeding step) or add a `simplehealth://e2e-health-seed-injury` dev deep link beside `e2e-health-set-note` |
| **A single run cannot draw a multi-day trend line** (TREND-107, BODY-104) | The weight line and every body-metric line plot one point per logged DAY and need two, but every row a flow writes lands today — a run cannot age a second day into existence, and back-dating would be writing test data the UI has no way to create. Those legs therefore branch: chart-and-legend when history exists, explanatory sentence when it does not. | ~~**The Home list has only TWO reachable scroll positions**~~ → **IT WAS AN APP DEFECT** | **RE-DIAGNOSED AND FIXED 2026-07-26.** The previous entry recorded this as an iOS-26 driver limitation: `scrollUntilVisible` timed out in both directions, `- scroll` did nothing, and `swipe` produced byte-identical screenshots. All of that was reproduced — and then explained. `HealthHomeScreen` mounted TWO `InputAccessoryView`s **unconditionally** on iOS. Under the New Architecture that component renders NOTHING (the "Done" bar never appeared above the decimal pad, which is why `home-keyboard-accessories.yaml` failed on `health-weight-keyboard-done` "is visible"), while the mounted native accessory container SWALLOWED THE PAN GESTURE for the whole screen. Taps went through normally, which is what made it look like a scroll-only driver fault. The controlled experiment: with the two elements commented out and nothing else changed, `scrollUntilVisible` COMPLETED on the first attempt and the screenshots differed. **This was member-facing, not just a harness problem — nobody could scroll their own Home tab past AT A GLANCE.** Fixed by replacing both bars with ordinary rows inside their own cards, shown while the field owns the keyboard (same testIDs, same behaviour, and they now actually render), plus `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets` on the list so the keypad no longer covers the row that summoned it — the log row sat at y≈569pt with the keypad's top edge at ≈568pt | **FIXED** in `src/features/health/screens/HealthHomeScreen.tsx`; `HEALTH-HOME-067`, `-067b`, `-068`, `-069`, `-070`, `-070b` rewritten against the shipped rows. A RESIDUAL driver flake remains — see the row below |
| **Maestro still cannot drive the Home ScrollView RELIABLY** | With the defect above fixed the list DOES scroll — but not on every attempt. Measured repeatedly on 2026-07-26 with screenshots after every step: one `swipe` moved the list to its second rest position (WATER and TODAY'S NOTE both clear of the floating tab bar, verified visually) and subsequent swipes were no-ops; on the next launch the identical command produced five byte-identical screenshots. `scrollUntilVisible` never drives this list in either direction, on any attempt, although it drives every `HealthSectionScreen` tab and More. `focus-health-home.yaml` now retries the gesture up to eight times and stops as soon as the target is visible; the closing `assertVisible` is still hard. Six Home flows (`home-weight-water-note`, `home-rapid-water`, `home-note-and-water-edges`, `home-keyboard-accessories`, `home-cancel-drafts`, `scroll-note-keyboard`) still fail on `health-water-plus` / `health-note-input` "is visible" because the retries do not converge | **OPEN — driver, and the iPad hypothesis is now RULED OUT.** Evidence: `/tmp/probe*` screenshot sets and the `~/.maestro/tests/2026-07-26_*` artefacts. **The taller viewport was tried on 2026-07-26 and does not fix it.** `Health-iPad` was created, the app installed, and `home-weight-water-note` run against it: 52 commands completed, **0 assertion failures**, and the run then died on `Request for viewHierarchy failed` with an empty body — the same XCUITest driver fault seen on the iPhone, so it is not viewport-dependent. Two `SimRenderServer` crash reports were filed the same day. **The distinction that matters: zero of these failures are assertion failures.** Nothing has failed on app behaviour since the `InputAccessoryView` fix; the driver stops answering. Independently of Maestro, a direct two-swipe probe on the fixed build moved the list and put WATER, TODAY'S NOTE and RECENT ACTIVITY on screen (before/after screenshot md5s differ; before the fix four swipes produced byte-identical bytes). So the app scrolls and the harness is what is unreliable. Remaining options, none tried: drive the gesture with `idb` instead of the XCUITest driver, or pin a Maestro/Xcode version pair known good on iOS 26. The controls themselves are proven at the unit layer (`HealthHomeScreen.test.tsx`, 100% of the file) |
| ~~**`focus-health-home.yaml`'s contract had drifted from every one of its callers**~~ | **CLOSED 2026-07-26.** The subflow took `FOCUS_ID` + **`LIFT_ID`** and swiped until `LIFT_ID` came on screen. **Every caller passes `FOCUS_ID` + `HOME_ANCHOR`.** An unset Maestro variable is not an error, so `LIFT_ID` was always undefined, the conditional swipe block never fired, and the subflow silently degraded to "scroll to the top and hope". That alone accounts for NINE of the ten Home-block failures on the baseline run. Worse, it had hidden a second bug: because the subflow was a no-op, nobody noticed that **seven call sites passed the WRONG anchor** — `health-water-plus` and `health-note-input` paired with `HOME_ANCHOR: top`, where they are a screen away | Subflow rewritten to the `HOME_ANCHOR` contract; all seven pairings corrected across `home-note-and-water-edges`, `home-rapid-water`, `home-weight-water-note`, `privacy-cross-user-leak` and `privacy-local-mutations` |
| ~~**`hideKeyboard` is intermittently a hard failure**~~ → **IT IS THE KEYBOARD TYPE, NOT LUCK** | **ROOT-CAUSED AND CLOSED 2026-07-26.** It was never intermittent. Maestro's `hideKeyboard` fails on this build's **decimal pad** and succeeds on the **text** keyboard, and every numeric field in this feature is `decimal-pad` on iOS. The controlled reproduction is inside a single flow: `nutrition-meal-detail-tools.yaml` dismissed the text keyboard with `hideKeyboard` after typing a food NAME (`COMPLETED`), then failed two steps later on the same screen, same driver, same run, after typing a NUMBER — `Couldn't hide the keyboard. This can happen if the app uses a custom input or doesn't expose a standard dismiss action.` That is the whole of it: the flows that "pass with it" are the ones whose last field before a button is text, and `activity-log-workout` types `7200` into `health-steps-input` and then calls it. It fails the flow rather than degrading, so a wrong guess here costs a whole surface | **FIXED** — `subflows/dismiss-health-keypad.yaml`. Two optional dismissals, neither of which can hard-fail: the floating "Done" pill iOS draws above a keyboard with no return key (verified present in a failure screenshot, and the tap logs `COMPLETED` on the numeric keyboard where `hideKeyboard` logged `FAILED`), then a tap on a caller-supplied non-interactive label — every section ScrollView carries `keyboardShouldPersistTaps="handled"`, which is the RN contract that makes a tap on non-touchable content dismiss the keyboard. Adopted by `activity-log-workout.yaml` and both new nutrition flows; the remaining call sites are text-keyboard-only and are left alone until they are next touched
| **Maestro's `enabled:` selector does not match a disabled RN `Pressable`** | React Native maps `disabled` / `accessibilityState.disabled` to an accessibility TRAIT, and on this build Maestro's `enabled: false` filter matched nothing — `assertVisible: {id: health-habit-add-button, enabled: false}` failed against a button that was plainly disabled. Every disabled-state assertion in the new flows is therefore BEHAVIOURAL: press the control and prove the side effect did not happen (pressing a disabled `Pressable` is a no-op). That is a stronger test than the attribute anyway. Two pre-existing flows still use the selector — `recipes-build-and-scale.yaml:97` and `fridge-stock-and-expiry.yaml:130` | Convert those two the same way when they are next touched, or confirm they were never reached |

Those three failures are NOT product or flow defects and must not be triaged as such | Run the suite against a quiesced tree (or a `git worktree` with its own Metro port). `launch-health.yaml` now retries the dev-chrome dismissal inside its wait rather than blocking on one 120 s `extendedWaitUntil`, which recovers the common case |

Home's tagline is now `Track your wellness at your own pace — your data is private to you.` (was `… your data stays private on this device.`); the first PRIVACY row is `Private to your account` (was `Stored on this device only`); and More's storage row is titled **Where your data lives** with a body that no longer contains "on this device only". The flows asserted the OLD, retracted wording — i.e. they were pinning a privacy claim the product had deliberately withdrawn | **CLOSED 2026-07-26** — `home-empty-and-privacy.yaml`, `scroll-note-keyboard.yaml` and `more-settings-controls.yaml` now assert the shipped wording, and the More leg asserts the two claims that still hold (`never shared with other Symply apps`, `never sent to an AI provider`) rather than the removed one |
| **The simulator's render server is crashing every 3–5 minutes** | `~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports` collected **14 `SimRenderServer` crash reports on 2026-07-25**, eight of them inside the two hours of the baseline runs (22:58, 23:02, 23:09, 23:13, 23:18, 23:21, 23:23, 23:29). When it goes down the app renders a blank white window, the dev client logs `bundle scheme is file - unable to connect to sharedPackageConnection`, and Maestro reports either a 120 s `health-home-screen` timeout or `App crashed or stopped while executing flow` — with **no app crash report**, because the app did not crash. `xcrun simctl terminate` + `launch` restores it immediately, which is the check that distinguishes this from a product fault | Host-level, not app-level. Treat any blank-app or `App crashed or stopped` failure as environmental until a `SymplyEcosystem-*.ips` of the same minute proves otherwise. `launch-health.yaml` now relaunches up to three times inside its wait instead of blocking on one 120 s `extendedWaitUntil`, which rides out most of them |
| **`scrollUntilVisible` does not drive these lists on a long hop — and that reframes the Home entry above** | **MEASURED 2026-07-26, on the Nutrition tab, with the driver freshly restarted.** `scrollUntilVisible: {element: {id: health-meal-group-lunch-toggle}, direction: DOWN, timeout: 30000}` — a target two and a half screens down — ran its full thirty seconds and moved the list **zero pixels** (the screenshots before and after are byte-identical), then failed "element not found". Two plain `- swipe` commands issued seconds later, **same session, same driver, same screen**, carried the same list from the day stepper past all four meal groups to the WATER card. So the GESTURE works and the command wrapping it does not. Short hops (about one screen) do succeed, which is why the suite has been able to use it at all and why this has always looked intermittent — the failures cluster on long hops, and a Health section tab is several screens tall. **What this means for the Home row above:** the Home list is not uniquely undriveable. `scrollUntilVisible` is unreliable on RN New-Architecture ScrollViews on this build generally, and Home is simply the tallest list reached from the coldest start. The `InputAccessoryView` defect was real and separate — it made Home unscrollable **by any means, including raw swipes and for real members**; this is the residue that was left after it was fixed | **FIXED for the flows this pass owns** — `subflows/health-swipe-section-to.yaml` repeats the raw gesture and stops as soon as the target is on screen, closing on a HARD `assertVisible`. Adopted by both nutrition flows, `trends-ranges.yaml`, `subflows/health-scroll-nutrition-top.yaml` and the new sweep. The other section flows still use `scrollUntilVisible` and still pass; convert them as they are next touched |
| ~~**`trends-ranges` cannot reach a testID the app renders**~~ | **DIAGNOSED AND FIXED 2026-07-26, and it was a stale flow, not a driver fault and not an app defect.** Checked for an always-mounted native element first, because the Home defect had exactly that shape: `HealthTrendsScreen` renders through `HealthSectionScreen` with **no** `Modal`, no `KeyboardAvoidingView` and no `InputAccessoryView` — the only two `InputAccessoryView` mentions left in `src/` are the comments in the already-fixed `HealthHomeScreen`. The real cause is that the flow asserted `health-trends-weight-latest` **with no scroll at all**. Those three tiles used to be the first thing under the range picker; the parity rebuild put `HealthWeightDashboard` there — a chart, a legend, an axis note and a mode toggle — and pushed them a screen down. The flow was written against the old card order and had been asserting an element that renders perfectly, just off-screen | **FIXED** — the weight tiles are now reached with `health-swipe-section-to.yaml` (not `scrollUntilVisible`, per the row above), and all five scrolls in the flow were converted. `HEALTH-TREND-101 … 104` are unchanged in intent |
| **A flow that dies mid-run poisons the next one, when rows are matched by name** | `continueOnFailure` is on and an iOS-26 driver drop is routine, so an aborted mutation flow leaves its rows behind. The nutrition flows match a row's verbs on the row testID AND its accessibility label together (the id carries a server-minted uuid), so a second row with the same name makes every per-row tap ambiguous — and one aborted run then poisons the flow until somebody cleans the account by hand. Observed for real: two `Maestro Diary One` rows, 520 kcal, from two aborted runs | **FIXED** — `subflows/health-clear-maestro-meals.yaml`, called on entry by both nutrition flows (and on both days by the copy flow). It deletes only rows whose name starts with `Maestro `, so a row a member logged on the shared account is never touched. The sweep walks the list by swipe for the reason in the row above — a scroll-based first attempt silently found nothing and reported success, which is how the two duplicates survived a sweep in the first place. Worth generalising: any suite that selects by a name it writes needs this |
| Network-payload assertions | PRIV-018/020/021 need Metro ring-buffer / PostHog / Sentry inspection, which Maestro cannot do | Unit-level client mock asserting no health fields |
| **`food-library-manage.yaml`'s own `HEALTH-FOOD-112` teardown likely taps nothing** | Found 2026-07-29 while reading the flow for convention (not running it). The leg deletes a diary row it logged via `id: 'health-food-delete-.*'` on the Nutrition screen — but that id pattern only exists on `HealthFoodLibraryScreen`'s own list rows (confirmed by grep); the diary's real per-entry delete id is `health-meal-delete-<id>` (`HealthMealDetail.tsx`), the pattern every other flow that deletes a diary row already uses correctly. Likely a copy-paste from the Foods-screen delete selector | Fix the selector to `health-meal-delete-.*` on the next touch of this flow; confirm on a device whether the tap currently no-ops or times out |

| 2026-08-13 | **Food Challenges: a whole surface that had NO coverage of any kind is now scored at three layers — new `CHAL` section, 95 rows, written in the SAME edit as the tests they cite.** Found by counting screens and store modules against test files rather than trusting this log — the same method that caught the `GOALS` hole on 2026-07-31, and the same finding: `HealthChallengesScreen.tsx` (490 lines, 15 testIDs) was named by **no** Jest suite and driven by **no** Maestro flow, and `healthChallengesStorage.ts` (282 lines) had no suite either. It was reached only indirectly through `HealthTodayChallengesCard.test.tsx`, which mocks `loadChallengeProgressToday` outright — so not one pure helper, and not one real `readThrough`/`writeThrough`, had ever executed under test. **New:** `src/features/health/__tests__/healthChallengesStorage.test.ts` (37 cases — pure/wire/offline, the three-layer shape `healthFridgeStorage.test.ts` uses), `src/features/health/screens/__tests__/HealthChallengesScreen.test.tsx` (40 cases), `e2e/maestro/health/challenges-create-edit-delete.yaml` + `subflows/go-health-challenges.yaml` (`CHAL-101…118`), and `HealthFeaturesScreen.test.tsx` (21 cases — the admin switchboard, the OTHER screen with no test; `platform.featureFlags.test.ts` owned the catalog and resolver but never the screen, so its redirect gate — the only thing between a common user and a working editor whose writes persist — was unpinned). All 98 pass; the suite is 3550 green / 6 pre-existing red. **The rule the store turned out to hinge on:** `isChallengeOnTrack` grades a week against its ELAPSED share, `(dayOfWeek / 7) × 0.8`, so a Monday needs ~11% and only a Sunday needs the full 80%. A regression to a flat `0.8` would pass every render test and tell almost every member they were "Behind" until Saturday — `CHAL-020…023` pin both ends of the ramp. **Registered, not just written:** the flow is in `config.yaml` `flowsOrder` (an unlisted flow silently never runs — `HEALTH-SUITE-003`), and `healthSuiteCompleteness` passes, so every testID it drives is proven to exist in app source. **NOT behind `needs-admin-feature-seed`:** `health-challenges` is absent from `src/config/healthFeatures.ts` entirely, so no flag gates it for a common user — unlike the 26 flows in that category. It owns no tab either; the Home FOOD CHALLENGES pencil is the only account-state-independent way in, because `HealthTodayChallengesCard` renders nothing until a challenge already exists. **One deliberate gap, recorded rather than faked (`CHAL-119`):** pause/resume is undriven on device because `health-challenge-toggle-*` carries the label `Pause challenge` on EVERY row, with no per-row token — on the shared account there is no way to address the row this flow owns without risking pausing another member's. It is pinned at the unit layer (`CHAL-085…088`) and the fix is a one-line label change. **Not fixed here, and not caused here:** the 6 pre-existing failures the baseline run surfaced — `voice.posture.test.ts` (×3), `habits.detail.test.tsx` HEALTH-HABIT-411, and `HealthStatTiles`/`HealthScaleRow` (both glyph-count assertions receiving 3 icons where 1 is expected, which looks like one shared `Icon`-rendering change rather than two coincidences). Reported, left for a pass that owns those files. |

| 2026-08-01 | **Weekly Trends widget — device-verified follow-up to the entry below, with a real label-collision bug found and fixed, `HEALTH-HOME-191` confirmed passing, and a full Health Maestro suite run surfacing unrelated pre-existing issues.** A booted simulator became available mid-session, so the "source-verified only" chart work below was actually driven on-device. **Found live, not caught by unit tests:** the per-point `showValues` label near either axis edge still crowded a gridline or the x-axis date row when shifted — root cause was `pointLabelShiftY`'s edge-zone threshold (0.18, i.e. 18% of the axis span) being narrower than a real case needed (a point sitting 21% below the ceiling still landed its label on the next gridline down); widened to 0.22 in `AppLineChart.tsx`, confirmed by screenshot diff (the "390"/"30 Jul" character-level overlap is gone). Also widened `initialSpacing` from 12pt to 24pt whenever `showValues` is set, so the FIRST point's label clears the y-axis tick-label column instead of crowding it (a horizontal, not vertical, collision the edge-zone fix alone couldn't address). Confirmed the fix is real, not a stale-bundle illusion, by fetching Metro's bundle directly (`curl .../node_modules/expo-router/entry.bundle?...`) and by a temporary on-screen text marker — the first two "it looks fixed" readings during this pass were actually still-stale renders. **`HEALTH-HOME-191` (added below) had a real bug of its own**, found running it for real: the Maestro leg checked the `chart-combined`/`chart-combined-empty` visible/notVisible branch on the SAME frame as the preceding `tapOn`, racing the re-render — fixed with an `extendedWaitUntil` on the wrapper (`chart-combined`) before branching on its child (`chart-combined-empty`), documented in the flow file's own comment. (2) Water-goal-default mismatches (`"/ 8 cups" is visible` failing across `home-empty-and-privacy`, `home-note-and-water-edges`, `water-cross-surface-parity`) — the shared account's saved goal is 6, not the fresh-account default of 8; account data drift. (3) `health-weight-latest-value` mismatches across six flows (`home-weight-water-note`, `home-weight-keyboard-return`, `privacy-local-mutations`, `home-weight-entry-lifecycle`, `trends-charts`, `privacy-cross-user-leak`) — the shared account's weight log was mutated repeatedly today by concurrent sessions and by this pass's own manual device exploration; not a code defect. | 2026-08-01 | **Weekly Trends widget: chart scale/label legibility fixed, plus a new combined-and-normalized view.** User-reported: the weight mini-chart's y-axis ran 0→ceiling regardless of `baselineValue`'s truncation working as designed, because a fractional floor (`min - 1`) produced a `niceLineMax` span whose ticks landed on ugly repeating decimals — root cause was the AXIS MATH, not the truncation logic (`chartReferenceLine.test.tsx`'s CHART-BASE-00x suite already proved truncation itself works). **Fixed in `HealthWeeklyTrendsWidget.tsx`:** the weight chart's floor is now `Math.floor(min) - 1` (a whole number, so ticks read as clean one-decimal figures); both mini-charts gained per-point `showValues` labels (gated at `Chart.maxLabelledBars`, the same 7-item threshold `AppBarChart` uses) so the exact kcal/weight figure sits on the line rather than relying on a squint at a coarse gridline; calorie axis ticks, the goal reference label and the kcal stat-row figures all gained `.toLocaleString()` thousands separators; a previously-dropped disclosure caption for the truncated weight axis was restored (`AppLineChart`'s own doc comment mandates this whenever `baselineValue` is used). **Then, per a follow-up request ("make Calories and Weight clickable to show both on the same chart, apply normalization; move labels to the bottom"):** the two legend keys are now `Pressable` and toggle a THIRD view — one combined chart with both series rescaled to a shared 0–100% axis (new `combinedNormalizedPoints` in `healthWeeklyTrendStorage.ts`, only for days that logged BOTH calories and weight — a day missing either has nothing to compare, so it is skipped rather than interpolated, the same "gap, not zero" rule the existing `caloriesChartPoints`/`weightChartPoints` already follow). **Shared `AppLineChart` gained three additive, backward-compatible capabilities to support this, all opt-in with unchanged defaults:** `AppLinePoint.displayValue` (a point's real reading, when it differs from the plotted/normalized `value`, for the "show values" label); `axisMax` on both the component and `lineAxis` (an exact ceiling that bypasses `niceLineMax`'s headroom padding — 100 stays 100, not 125 — for a domain that is already fixed and known, like a percentage scale); `formatPointValue`/`formatPointValue2` (per-series point-label formatters independent of the shared axis-tick `formatValue`, since a normalized chart's axis reads "%" while its two series' labels must still read as kcal and weight, two never-interchangeable numbers); and `valueLabelPosition: 'above' | 'below'` (a new `Chart.pointValueShiftYBelow` token) — applied to all three chart instances in this widget, addressing the "labels look weird at the top" complaint (they were crowding the goal rule and the top gridlines). **Tests:** `src/components/ui/__tests__/AppLineChart.test.tsx` + `chartReferenceLine.test.tsx` (32 tests, unchanged, confirming the additions are non-breaking); `healthWeeklyTrendStorage.test.ts` gained 4 tests for `combinedNormalizedPoints` (rescale correctness, drops a day missing either measurement, a flat week sits mid-scale rather than dividing by zero, empty/no-overlap input); new `HealthWeeklyTrendsWidget.test.tsx` (5 tests: default two-chart layout, legend tap → combined chart, tap again → split back apart, the no-overlap empty state) — this widget had zero component-level Jest coverage before this pass. **Matrix + Maestro:** new row `HEALTH-HOME-191` (numbered out of sequence, same non-renumbering convention as HOME-138/145/146) added to `home.md` and to `home-dashboard-widgets-calorie-trends-challenges.yaml` — legend tap combines, chart-or-empty branch, tap again splits apart. **Source-verified only, same as every recent addition to this file — no simulator available this session.** **Pre-existing, confirmed unrelated:** the same 5-10 "Customise Home" toggle/reorder/reset failures documented in the two entries below (`health-home-customise-toggle` and friends) recur across `HealthHomeScreen.test.tsx`, `home.dashboard.test.tsx` and `HealthWeightScreen.test.tsx` — untouched by this pass, confirmed present before it started. |

Per-area detail (new rows, ID ranges, defects) lives in sixteen `matrix-<area>.md` deltas pending a full structural merge into this file's per-section tables; this entry is the changelog-level summary in the meantime. **Defects found and FIXED this pass:** (1) **`?limit=<fraction>` 500ed** on 14 GET routes across `health.ts`, `health-assets.ts`, `health-exercises.ts`, `health-body-extras.ts`, `health-social.ts`, `health-food.ts` — a client-supplied REAL bound straight into drizzle's `.limit()` hit D1's `SQLITE_MISMATCH` on an otherwise ordinary authenticated list; all 14 now reject a non-integer `limit` with 400 (`health-social-authz.test.ts`'s `HEALTH-SOCIAL-DEFECT-01` pin retired, moved into its `JUNK` sweep). (2) **A repeat DELETE re-stamped the tombstone and answered 200 instead of 404** on all seven soft-deleters — `deleteWeight`, `deleteWater`, `deleteNutrition`, `deleteMeasurement`, `deleteHealthEntry`, `deleteHabit`, `removePeriodDay` — none guarded `deleted_at IS NULL` in their WHERE, unlike the edit-path functions in the same file; all seven now do (`HEALTH-CYCLE-182`, `HEALTH-API-040`, and the weight service's repeat-delete spec updated to expect the corrected 404/`false`). (3) **The challenge leaderboard's defence-in-depth ignored the grant's own `relationship_type`** — `filterByGrant` asked "family OR buddy still live" where `readSharedMetrics` correctly asks "the grant's OWN type still live", so two users who are both family and buddies could keep seeing each other on a stale FAMILY grant after leaving the family. `filterByGrant` now carries `relationship_type` through its query; the paired characterisation test flips from "still honoured (weaker half)" to a regression pin on the fix. (4) **At either water-cup clamp bound, a tap silently wrote to the server while the display stayed pinned** — `adjustWater` POSTed/undid regardless of whether the optimistic count actually moved, so a `+1` at the 30-cup ceiling grew the real total while the ring stayed at 30, and the next "Remove a cup" looked dead. `adjustWater` now no-ops (no network call at all) when the clamped count does not change (`HEALTH-STORE-069`, `078` rewritten from characterisation to regression pins). (5) **The Health Watch face had no sign-in gate at all** — unlike the widget (which already correctly checks household presence via `isSignedIn`, never the legacy `isAuthenticated`), `SymplyHealthWatchView.load()` read `watch_health_today` unconditionally; `AppGroup.clearAuth()` drops `current_household_id` on logout but never touches that snapshot key, so a signed-out watch could keep rendering a previous sign-in's real health data indefinitely. Fixed to gate on `AppGroup.getHouseholdId()`, matching the widget's pattern; new Tier-1 Jest guard `HEALTH-WATCH-004` reads the Swift source and pins the gate + its ordering ahead of the decode. (6) **A backend test fixture was 27 columns stale** — `health-test-helpers.ts`'s `body_measurements` DDL still had only the 14 pre-0131 legacy columns, so any test touching a comprehensive body site (`left_arm_mid` etc.) 500ed with `no column named…`; fixed by widening the fixture to match migration 0131 (confirmed separately: 0131 IS applied on both staging and production D1 — its own header comment claiming otherwise is stale documentation, not a live gap). (7) **A fridge quantity input could silently block Save with no explanation** — a lone decimal separator or an over-1,000,000 value is sanitizer-legal but parser-illegal, and the only inline hint on the form was expiry-side; added a matching `health-fridge-quantity-hint`. **Also fixed, not a defect:** `app.config.ts`'s `NSMicrophoneUsageDescription` and the `react-native-webrtc` plugin's `microphonePermission` hardcoded "talk to Aihousekeeper hands-free in voice mode" for EVERY brand's build (this plugin set is shared, unfiltered by brand) — a Symply Health/Budget/Kaizen/Language build asked for microphone access to reach a House-only feature; now brand-neutral, matching the sibling `expo-speech-recognition` plugin's existing copy style. `healthCacheKeys.test.ts`'s file-scanner had a blind spot: `HEALTH_OUTBOX_KEY` (a real, necessary clear-on-logout key — the offline write queue) lives in `healthRepository.ts`, which the scanner's `*Storage.ts`/`healthKit.ts` filter didn't cover, so `HEALTH-CACHE-002` couldn't have caught a genuinely stale entry in that class of file; widened rather than the regex loosened, so an unrelated file still can't opt itself in. **Deployed to staging + production** (`deploy:fleet`) for defects (1) and the `health-assets.ts` single-site precursor to it. **Reported, not fixed (yours to prioritise):** re-enabling health reminders after a full data wipe restores the pre-wipe values instead of defaults (`health-reminders-service.ts` `savePreferences`'s `onConflictDoUpdate` clears the tombstone unconditionally while only setting the patched columns) — a partial-merge-semantics product decision, not a one-line fix. |
| 2026-07-26 | **Three new flows close the three largest control gaps, and a suite-wide keyboard defect that could have taken most of the Health suite down is fixed.** New: `vitality-scales-and-toggles.yaml` (`HEALTH-VITALITY-120 … 134`) takes `HealthVitalityScreen` **5/18 → 18/18**; `activity-goals-and-session-edit.yaml` (`HEALTH-ACT-120 … 133`) takes `HealthActivityScreen` **7/14 → 14/14`; `cycle-length-notes-history.yaml` (`HEALTH-CYCLE-130 … 139`) takes `HealthCycleScreen` **4/9 → 9/9**. Each is a SPLIT of an existing screen's flow, not an extension — the same rule the Weight and Nutrition pairs follow, so a mid-run driver drop costs half a screen. Four existing flows gained the cheap remainder: the cm/in toggle (`HEALTH-BODY-109`), the exercise favourite in both directions (`HEALTH-EXER-116`), and the weight chart's Trend/Change and average-line toggles (`HEALTH-WEIGHT-437`/`438`). **Control→flow coverage 181/260 (70%) → 226/260 (87%)**, with twelve components now at 100%. Every leg covers STATE, not a happy tap: ten scale rows at two levels each and one at BOTH ends of its range, five checkbox rows flipped ON and OFF, two Alerts picked / **cancelled** / restored, a range picker across all three options, a workout edit **abandoned** and then **saved**, a note field written and **emptied**, a kegel figure **above the ceiling** and then **blank**, and every disabled control proven behaviourally. The strongest assertions are the derived ones — `energyScore = level * 10` and `mentalScore = clarity*5 + mood*5 - (stress-5)*3` are pure functions of controls on the Vitality screen, so the flow predicts `Energy: 40` / `Mental: 70` and then `Energy: 70` / `Mental: 55` exactly. **The defect found and fixed:** the 2026-07-26 `hideKeyboard` root cause was right but only three flows had been converted, and the entry claimed the rest were "text-keyboard-only". Resolving every `- hideKeyboard` back to the field it follows and that field back to its `keyboardType` found **21 of 49 sites on a numeric keyboard**, across ELEVEN flows including `vitality-log-and-score`, `weight-tab-log-and-edit`, `weight-tab-dashboard-controls`, `nutrition-log-meal`, `trends-charts`, `food-library-manage`, `recipes-build-and-scale`, `fridge-stock-and-expiry`, `body-measure-and-trend`, `more-overflow-body-habits` and `exercises-detail-log`. `hideKeyboard` fails the FLOW, so each was a whole surface lost; all eighteen `decimal-pad`/`number-pad` sites now route through `subflows/dismiss-health-keypad.yaml` with a verified non-touchable anchor. Also: the two surviving `enabled:` selectors converted to behavioural proofs, and 21 row-verb taps in the three P2 flows qualified with their testID pattern as well as their label. **Two counter defects corrected and disclosed** — the base half of a backward id was never credited, and a base arriving through `idPrefix` rather than `testID` was invisible; both are stated with before/after numbers on each basis so the earned delta is separable from the correction. `src/features/health` held at **100% / 100% / 100% / 100%** (1825 tests, 44 suites); `healthSuiteCompleteness` 3/3; all 80 Health YAML files parse |
| 2026-07-26 | **The Nutrition diary verbs — the largest remaining control gap — closed, plus two long-standing "driver" failures root-caused and one member-facing app defect.** Two new flows: `nutrition-meal-detail-tools.yaml` (`HEALTH-NUTR-170 … 188`) owns the PER-ROW verbs and the quick-add grid; `nutrition-copy-and-batch.yaml` (`HEALTH-NUTR-190 … 209`) owns the copy panels and the multi-select bar. Split deliberately — one flow covering both would be long enough that a mid-run driver drop would cost the whole diary surface. Both cover STATES, not happy taps: empty and zero calories refused **in place** with the editor still open, a portion of `0` and of `1001` (the route's `max(1000)`, mirrored client-side) both refused before the wire, the three quick-add tabs driven across all three options, the copy source picker across three, both steppers in both directions, a batch delete **cancelled and then confirmed**, both copy panels **dismissed and then submitted**, and every disabled state proven behaviourally because Maestro's `enabled:` selector does not match a disabled RN `Pressable` on this build. Control→flow coverage **156/252 (62%) → 181/261 (69%)**; `HealthMealDetail` 1/15 → 14/15, `HealthMealTools` 3/12 → 12/12, `HealthQuickAdd` 0/7 → 4/7, `HealthNutritionScreen` 9/18 → 18/19. **App defect fixed:** `HealthSectionScreen` lacked `automaticallyAdjustKeyboardInsets`, so on all twelve section tabs the decimal pad was drawn OVER the field that summoned it — the same defect fixed on Home days earlier and never generalised to the shared container. **Two "driver" failures root-caused, both of which had been recorded as flaky:** (1) `hideKeyboard` does not fail at random, it fails on the **decimal pad** and succeeds on the **text** keyboard — proven inside one flow, one run, one driver, where it did both eleven seconds apart; that is the whole of `activity-log-workout`'s failure, and the fix is `subflows/dismiss-health-keypad.yaml`. (2) `scrollUntilVisible` moved the Nutrition list **zero pixels** over a full 30 s timeout on a long hop while two raw `- swipe` commands seconds later carried it to the bottom — so the gesture works and the command does not, which means the Home list is not uniquely undriveable and `subflows/health-swipe-section-to.yaml` is the general answer. (3) `trends-ranges` was neither: `HealthTrendsScreen` has no always-mounted native element (checked first, because the Home defect had that shape), and the flow was simply asserting the weight tiles with no scroll at all after the parity rebuild pushed them a screen down behind `HealthWeightDashboard`. **One re-runnability trap closed:** rows matched by a name the flow writes are poisoned by an aborted run leaving duplicates behind — observed for real — so both flows sweep `Maestro *` rows on entry. `src/features/health` held at **100% / 100% / 100% / 100%** (1825 tests, 44 suites); `healthSuiteCompleteness` 3/3; all 77 Health YAML files parse |
| 2026-07-26 | **Backend Health surface coverage pass — new `BEHARDEN` section (~60 rows), and FOUR product defects found and fixed.** The Worker side of Health had never been coverage-measured. Working down the uncovered-branch list for `routes/health*.ts`, `services/health*.ts` and `services/health-ai/**` took it from **93.9% statements / 88.6% branches** to **97.9% / 94.5%** (code files, excluding the drizzle table declarations whose index callbacks drizzle-orm never invokes at runtime), and every defect below came out of a branch that had simply never been executed. **(1) An edit could land on a TOMBSTONE.** `updateWeight` and `updateNutrition` looked up by `(id, user_id)` with no `deleted_at` guard, so a member's own deleted row answered **200 with a body** while an unknown id and another user's id both answered 404 — a third signal for a prober — and the edit re-stamped `updated_at` on a dead row, which reads on a second device as a value moving on an entry that is gone. `reportionNutrition` and `readOwnedEntry` already documented the rule in their own comments (`HEALTH-PRIV-030…033`). **(2) The coach COMMIT route trusted its caller.** `payload_hash` is FNV-1a over a public encoding — a checksum, not a signature, as the module header says — so a client can mint one for any payload it likes; everything downstream then read `amount_ml`, `weight`, `unit` and `items` straight off that unverified object. A hand-made proposal could store `1e12` ml through a path whose own zod cap is 10 000, a numeric STRING into a REAL column (SQLite stores it as text and the next `total + amount_ml` concatenates), or a `unit` the CHECK constraint refuses — a 500 on user input. `validateCommit` now RE-DERIVES the payload through the same normaliser `buildProposal` uses and hands the derived copy on (`HEALTH-AI-400…407`, `157…158`). **(3) The coach ledger was written AFTER the diary**, the exact ordering its own comment warns against: any failure of the ledger write left a meal in the diary that nothing recorded, so the client's retry logged it a second time. It now claims a `pending` receipt first and confirms it after, and a retry FINISHES a pending one rather than reporting it already done (`HEALTH-AI-159`, `411`). **(4) `PUT /custom-foods/:id` did not patch.** The route declares all four `base_*_per_100` columns as independently optional, but the service gated on `base_calories_per_100` alone and then rebuilt the basis with the CREATE-time resolver — so a patch naming only the protein figure was silently DROPPED with a 200, and a patch naming only the energy figure silently ZEROED protein, carbs and fats, after which every portion derived from that row read 0 g (`HEALTH-FOOD-301…302`). **Also pinned for the first time:** the delta-sync cursor now has a completeness guard — every table migrations 0119–0128 added is either pulled by `/sync` or listed with a reason, in both directions (`HEALTH-SYNC-201`), which is the check that would have caught the earlier asymmetry where `/sync/push` accepted cycle settings, goals and the P2 rows that the pull did not return; `POST /nutrition/entries/bulk` and `POST /nutrition/copy-day`, both live in production, gained their first HTTP-level tests (`HEALTH-NUTR-210…215`); and the cycle re-anchor on deleting a period day (`HEALTH-CYCLE-120…124`) — whose behaviour this document previously described BACKWARDS in the CYCLE preamble, now corrected. **One dead branch DELETED rather than tested:** a `|| 1` divide-by-zero guard in `measurement-insight-service` that sat inside a `kept.length > 0` branch and could never fire. Two genuinely unreachable defence-in-depth arms in `routes/health-food.ts` are left in place with a comment saying why. Backend 214 files / 2817 tests → **216 / 2952**, no regression; two new suites (`coach-service.test.ts`, `measurement-insight-service.test.ts`) |
| 2026-07-26 | **Parity phase P3 — the AI surfaces. New `AI` section (173 rows).** The donor's three AI features landed behind the platform chokepoints: the **coach** (`routes/healthCoach.ts` → `/health/ai/coach/*` — turn, propose, commit, ledger, consent), the **nutrition-label and meal scanners** (`/ai/scan-nutrition-label` + `/ai/scan-product-name` folded into ONE call; `/ai/analyze-food` and `/ai/analyze-food-scale` folded into one prompt), and the **body-insight producer** `routes/health-body-extras.ts` has been waiting for since P2. Every model call goes through `ai/provider.ts`, is gated by `assertCanUseAI`, honours BYOK via `createAnthropicAdapterForUser`, and is rate-limited by a new DO-backed `health:ai` bucket registered on the APP ahead of every `/health` mount (`HEALTH-AI-190`, the P4 mount-order lesson applied before it could bite). **Migration `0128_health_ai.sql` is written and applied to NO D1.** **Three things the donor got wrong and this port does not:** its escalation matcher (11 emergency categories) runs only on the deterministic path, so on any account with AI configured it never fires — here it runs FIRST on every turn, before consent, entitlement or a token (`HEALTH-AI-120` is a hard negative on the provider mock); its coach auto-grants five consent scopes on first use with a comment admitting it is a placeholder — here no live receipt means the turn is refused before a health row is read (`113`); and its own "LLM may only wordsmith an already-approved structured result" rule is never applied to the insight surface — here `composeInsightSpeech` throws `ungrounded_number:<n>` and the producer DROPS what fails (`011`, `177`). **The coach cannot write:** it emits a proposal carrying an FNV-1a `payload_hash` (ported byte-for-byte from the donor's Swift `HealthCoachEncoding`), and the commit route re-hashes what it receives AND requires the client to echo the hash it displayed — `045`/`152` prove a tampered payload is refused even with a matching echo, `151` proves a double tap replays instead of logging twice. **Deliberately absent:** no conversation table (the donor does not persist transcripts either; the transcript lives under `health.coach.v1` and is cleared on sign-out), no `health_coach_facts` bitemporal memory (needs its own retention spec), no body-fat estimate or posture score on the produced insight (there is no photograph, and the Navy-formula number is derivable and deliberately not derived — `172`), and no on-device OCR fallback for the label scanner. **Two app-code changes outside the new files:** `FoodDraft.sourceType` now reaches the wire, so a scanned food is distinguishable from a typed one for the first time since 0120 shipped the enum value (`200`); and the AI coach came OFF Home's COMING SOON list, which `HEALTH-HOME-089` now asserts and one Maestro flow still contradicts — see Harness gaps. Backend 193 files/2328 tests → 201/2506 with no regression |

**Fridge is still missing one**. **Two honest limits recorded, not papered over:** the `avoid` injury gate cannot be seeded from the app (no injury-logging surface), so `EXER-111` / the refusal half of `EXER-113` are conditional legs that skip on a clear account; and a single run cannot produce the two logged DAYS a trend LINE needs, so `TREND-107` / `BODY-104` branch between chart and explanatory sentence while everything one day CAN produce is asserted hard |

| 2026-07-25 | **Migration `0124_health_entry_updates.sql` — three backend gaps closed, and the client workarounds they forced removed.** (1) A health entry could not be UPDATED: `/health/entries` was create + soft-delete only, so "edit a session" RE-RECORDED it — write the replacement, then tombstone the original — which kept the data safe but minted a new id and moved the logged time. `PUT /health/entries/:id` (the donor's own route, unported in P1) and a merging `PUT /health/entries/workouts/:id` now edit in place. (2) A workout had no INTENSITY, so it rode the note as a leading `[hard]` tag because the route's zod schema stripped every key it did not name; it is a real `health_entries.intensity` column now, with a closed enum that 400s a made-up value instead of silently dropping it. `parseWorkoutNote` survives in the STORE as a READ-ONLY reader — 0124's backfill lifts the tag into the column but deliberately does not rewrite the note (that would restamp every workout the user ever logged and force a full re-pull on every device), and a pre-0124 offline cache has no column at all. (3) A nutrition entry's PORTION could not be re-derived, because the per-100 basis and the source food were not stored: `nutrition_entries` gained `food_id` plus the donor's four `base_*_per_100` columns (donor `018_nutrition_base_values.sql`, column-for-column), and `POST /health/nutrition/entries/:id/portion` re-derives the macros server-side — always from the BASIS, never by scaling the figures already on the row, so repeated edits cannot drift. **+65 rows:** `ACT` `140` … `169` (30), `NUTR` `112` … `134` (23), `FOOD-140`; `ACT-058/059/060` were REWRITTEN in place because they described behaviour that no longer exists. **Client simplified:** `HealthActivityScreen` edits in place and its copy no longer apologises for a moved timestamp; `HealthNutritionScreen` stopped add-then-deleting (`updateMealEntry` had been exported and unused since P1) and a move between slots is now one call; `HealthMealDetail` gained a portion-only Rescale control, shown ONLY when the row carries a basis — offering one that can only ever 400 is worse than not offering it. **Two deliberate deviations, documented in the migration header:** no CHECK constraints on the new columns (SQLite cannot add one without a full table rebuild; zod rejects at the boundary with a 400 rather than a 500), and no FK on `nutrition_entries.food_id` — `custom_foods` is soft-deleted so an ON DELETE rule would never fire, and a diary row must KEEP its macros when the source food goes, because the meal was still eaten. **Not applied to any D1 and not deployed** — 0124 is left ready |
| 2026-07-25 | **Injury logging UI shipped — `INJ` 40 → 73 (+33) and a new `INJUI` section (17)**, closing UI_PARITY_AUDIT Tier-1 item #5. All six `/health/injuries*` routes had been deployed to staging and production with **zero RN callers**, so the workout library's injury gate — `injuryFlag`, the "Avoid for now" / "Take care" badges, the banner and the two-tap log guard — could only ever fire for a row entered outside the app. New client: `src/api/healthInjuries.ts`, `src/features/health/healthInjuryStorage.ts`, `src/features/health/screens/HealthInjuriesScreen.tsx`, route `app/(tabs)/health-injuries.tsx`, pool entry `health-injuries` (`defaultHidden`, brand icon `symptoms`). **The rows are weighted onto the one failure that is invisible at runtime:** `body_part` is free text on the wire, but the gate folds it through `canonicalBodyPart()`, which splits on NON-ALPHANUMERICS — so the donor's own `BodyPart.rawValue` (`leftKnee`) folds to `leftknee`, matches nothing, and the safety surface silently ceases to exist with no crash, no 400 and a screen that looks correct. The port therefore persists the donor's **`displayName`** (`Left Knee` → `knee`), and `HEALTH-INJ-041` … `047` prove it by reading the **deployed** alias and muscle tables out of `backend/src/services/health-exercise-service.ts` and the 88-row catalogue out of `0123_health_exercise_library.sql` at test time — a hand-copied constant would have happily agreed with itself. `INJ-045` is the negative control (the donor value misses, ours hits) and `INJ-043` pins the miss set at **exactly** `head` + both upper arms, so a silent safety hole and a stale user-facing disclaimer both fail the suite. **Three donor behaviours deliberately not ported, and pinned as such** (`INJ-050`, `INJUI-017`): the "Recovery Tips" card (`Professional recommendations`, `Consult a professional for persistent pain`, `Use RICE…`), the per-level interpretations (`Noticeable pain, may limit some activities`), and the clinical muscle-group picker (`ACL`, `MCL`, `Meniscus`) — an injury is medical-adjacent, and the app records what the user reports rather than reading it back at them. **Two places the port is better than the donor:** resolved injuries are visible and **reopenable** (`INJUI-014`; the donor filters healed rows out of every list, so a flare-up cannot be logged against the old entry), and the form is inline rather than one of the donor's six stacked `.sheet` modifiers. **Honest gap: no Maestro flow** — `e2e/maestro/health/` is owned by another agent, so `more-overflow-all-tabs.yaml` is now **three** hidden tabs behind `brand.cjs` (Fridge, Workouts, Injuries) rather than two. Activity preferences and body insights still have no client at all |
| 2026-07-25 | **P3 external food database (FatSecret) — `FOOD` +55 rows (`200` … `257`), execution groups 53–55.** P2 shipped `/foods/search` over the user's OWN library only, so there was no way to look up a food they had not already typed in; the donor's `foodSearch.ts` topped that up from FatSecret and `integrations.ts` held the raw passthroughs. The port routes ALL of it through one chokepoint, `backend/src/services/health-food-provider.ts`, on the `ai/provider.ts` model — **the device never holds a FatSecret credential and never calls FatSecret.** Search answers two arrays (`results` = library, `external` = provider) rather than the donor's one merged badge-tagged list, because a provider hit has none of a library row's verbs until `POST /foods/import` gives it one; that import is idempotent on the new `custom_foods.external_source` / `.external_id` pair (**migration `0127`, applied to no D1**) and re-derives the serving from the basis, so a repeat add never duplicates and never overwrites a correction the user made. **Three donor defects are asserted as fixed, not just avoided:** `FOOD-203` pins that a serving with no `metric_serving_amount` is NOT declared to be 100 g (the donor's `|| 100` made a 78 kcal egg 4x too low in every later portion), `FOOD-232` pins the duplicate-on-every-save that `UUID(uuidString:"fatsecret_123") ?? UUID()` guaranteed, and `FOOD-205` pins the `serving[0]` blind pick. **Fail-closed is the second theme:** four distinct provider states (`not_configured` / `rate_limited` / `unavailable` / `skipped`) each become their own sentence, and `FOOD-229` / `FOOD-252` assert the NEGATIVE directly — no provider body, HTTP code, host name, credential or status word reaches the response or the screen. `FOOD-227` proves a missing credential does not fail the search at all. **No test touches the real API:** `globalThis.fetch` is stubbed and recorded in both backend suites, which is what makes `FOOD-212` ("the secret only ever goes to the token endpoint") expressible. CONFIG_KV is real, so the 24 h search cache and the 60 s post-429 cooldown are asserted rather than mocked. **Two honest gaps recorded rather than papered over:** the credential is set nowhere (so the shipping behaviour today is the `not_configured` path, which is itself covered), and FatSecret's IP allowlist against a Worker's unstable egress IP cannot be confirmed until a real key exists — its rejection is FatSecret error code 21, mapped to `unavailable` by `FOOD-216`. There is **no Maestro leg**: `e2e/maestro/health/` is owned elsewhere and the feature cannot be driven on a device until the credential lands. One pre-existing platform bound surfaced and is documented rather than widened (`FOOD-204`): `validateBasis`'s ceilings are gram-semantic, so a per-serving basis cannot pass them — the same refusal a hand-typed "1 serving / 78 kcal" already gets from `POST /custom-foods` |
